=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N foods, each containing exactly one of vitamins 1, 2, and 3.
Specifically, eating the i-th food gives you A_i units of vitamin V_i, and C_i calories.
Takahashi can choose any subset of these N foods as long as the total calorie consumption does not exceed X.
Find the maximum possible value of this: the minimum intake among vitamins 1, 2, and 3.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N X
V_1 A_1 C_1
V_2 A_2 C_2
\vdots
V_N A_N C_N

Output

Print the maximum possible value of "the minimum intake among vitamins 1, 2, and 3" when the total calories consumed is at most X.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 5000
- 1 \leq X \leq 5000
- 1 \leq V_i \leq 3
- 1 \leq A_i \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq C_i \leq X
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5 25
1 8 5
2 3 5
2 7 10
3 2 5
3 3 10

Sample Output 1

3

Each food provides the following if eaten:

- 1st food: 8 units of vitamin 1, and 5 calories
- 2nd food: 3 units of vitamin 2, and 5 calories
- 3rd food: 7 units of vitamin 2, and 10 calories
- 4th food: 2 units of vitamin 3, and 5 calories
- 5th food: 3 units of vitamin 3, and 10 calories

Eating the 1st, 2nd, 4th, and 5th foods gives 8 units of vitamin 1, 3 units of vitamin 2, 5 units of vitamin 3, and 25 calories.
In this case, the minimum among the three vitamin intakes is 3 (vitamin 2).
It is impossible to get 4 or more units of each vitamin without exceeding 25 calories, so the answer is 3.

Sample Input 2

2 5000
1 200000 1
2 200000 1

Sample Output 2

0

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Read input:
import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N, X = map(int, input().split())
    dp = [[0]*(X+1) for _ in range(3)]
    for _ in range(N):
        v,a,c = map(int, input().split())
        arr = dp[v-1]
        # 0/1 knapsack max value for at most capacity
        # descending
        for w in range(X, c-1, -1):
            val = arr[w-c] + a
            if val > arr[w]:
                arr[w] = val
    d0,d1,d2 = dp
    ans = 0
    for i in range(X+1):
        di = d0[i]
        # optional if di <= ans? But d1/d2 maybe? min <= di, so if di <= ans, no improve. But if ans grows, many i skipped. However d0 nondecreasing, so if di <= ans, for all later i? Wait d0 nondecreasing, so if d0[i] <= ans, later d0 may become > ans. So cannot break, but can skip this i. If di <= ans, continue. This saves when d0 values small.
        if di <= ans:
            continue
        # Need consider j from 0 to X-i
        # d1[j] maybe <= ans skip? But if d1[j] <= ans, min <= ans, no improve. Since d1 nondecreasing, for j before crossing maybe skip. Could find first j with d1[j] > ans? But d2 decreases; maybe use loops.
        for j in range(X - i + 1):
            d1j = d1[j]
            if d1j <= ans:
                continue
            val = d2[X - i - j]
            if val > ans:
                m = di if di < d1j else d1j
                if val < m:
                    m = val
                if m > ans:
                    ans = m
    print(ans)

Implementation:
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    it = iter(data)
    N = next(it); X = next(it)
    dp = [[0]*(X+1) for _ in range(3)]
    cnt = [0]*3
    for _ in range(N):
        v = next(it); a = next(it); c = next(it)
        idx = v-1
        cnt[idx] += 1
        arr = dp[idx]
        # local variables for speed
        # for w in range(X, c-1, -1):
        #    nv = arr[w-c] + a
        #    if nv > arr[w]: arr[w] = nv
        # Could optimize by using while loop? for loop is fine.
        for w in range(X, c - 1, -1):
            nv = arr[w - c] + a
            if nv > arr[w]:
                arr[w] = nv
    if cnt[0] == 0 or cnt[1] == 0 or cnt[2] == 0:
        print(0)
        return
    d0, d1, d2 = dp
    ans = 0
    # Maybe compute upper bound? min(d0[X], d1[X], d2[X])? Actually answer <= min total max per vitamin with full X, but if allocate budgets, answer <= min(d0[X], d1[X], d2[X])? Since each vitamin budget <=X, max amount with full X is upper bound. Could set ans=0 and loops. Not needed.
    for i in range(X + 1):
        di = d0[i]
        if di <= ans:
            continue
        limit = X - i
        # local d1,d2, ans
        for j in range(limit + 1):
            d1j = d1[j]
            if d1j <= ans:
                continue
            d2v = d2[limit - j]
            # m = min(di, d1j, d2v)
            if d2v <= ans:
                continue
            m = di if di < d1j else d1j
            if d2v < m:
                m = d2v
            if m > ans:
                ans = m
    print(ans)

Feasibility function:
def feasible(m):
    if m == 0: return True
    total = 0
    for arr in dp:
        # first index c with arr[c] >= m
        # arr is nondecreasing length X+1
        # use bisect_left(arr, m)
        pos = bisect_left(arr, m)
        if pos > X: return False
        total += pos
        if total > X: return False
    return total <= X
Because b_v(m) = pos (0..X). If pos == X+1, impossible. Since arr length X+1, bisect_left returns X+1 if m > arr[-1]. Then infeasible.

Then binary search:
lo = 0
hi = min(dp[0][X], dp[1][X], dp[2][X]) + 1
while hi - lo > 1:
    mid = (lo + hi)//2
    if feasible(mid):
        lo = mid
    else:
        hi = mid
print(lo)

Let's write code:

import sys
from bisect import bisect_left

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, X = data[0], data[1]
    cnt = [0, 0, 0]
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        v = data[idx]
        cnt[v - 1] += 1
        idx += 3
    if cnt[0] == 0 or cnt[1] == 0 or cnt[2] == 0:
        print(0)
        return

    dp = [[0] * (X + 1) for _ in range(3)]
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        v = data[idx]
        a = data[idx + 1]
        c = data[idx + 2]
        idx += 3
        arr = dp[v - 1]
        # 0/1 knapsack: max vitamin amount with calorie budget <= w
        for w in range(X, c - 1, -1):
            val = arr[w - c] + a
            if val > arr[w]:
                arr[w] = val

    d0, d1, d2 = dp
    high = min(d0[X], d1[X], d2[X]) + 1
    # binary search
    def ok(m):
        total = 0
        for arr in (d0, d1, d2):
            pos = bisect_left(arr, m)
            if pos > X:
                return False
            total += pos
            if total > X:
                return False
        return True

    lo = 0
    # hi is infeasible because answer <= min(d0[X], d1[X], d2[X])
    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if ok(mid):
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    print(lo)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need check `pos > X` vs `pos == X+1`. bisect_left returns X+1 if m > arr[-1]. Since arr length X+1, pos can be X+1. `pos > X` works. If m=0, pos=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if the DP with at-most capacity and zeros can be replaced by exact capacity DP and prefix max; which one is more robust? Let's compare:
Exact:
dp = [-10**18]*(X+1); dp[0]=0
for item:
  for w in range(X, c-1, -1):
    if dp[w-c] != -inf:
      dp[w] = max(dp[w], dp[w-c]+a)
then prefix max: for w in 1..X: dp[w] = max(dp[w], dp[w-1])
This is also 25M loops but with -inf checks and prefix. At-most DP is faster and simpler. But is at-most DP with zeros and descending update always correct for 0/1? Let's prove with an example where old dp[w-c] might include the same item due to "at most" and descending? We already considered descending prevents using same item because w-c < w and not processed yet. But what if old dp[w-c] includes a combination whose cost is <= w-c, and adding item cost c yields cost <= w. It doesn't use the new item because old. Good. So correct.

Let's think about a potential counterexample for at-most DP:
Items: item1 cost 3 value 5, item2 cost 4 value 6. X=7.
Exact optimal at most 7: both cost7 value11.
At-most DP:
init 0.
item1: dp3..7=5.
item2 cost4:
w=7: val=dp3+6=11 -> dp7=11.
w=6: val=dp2+6=6 -> dp6=max(5,6)=6.
w=5: val=dp1+6=6 -> dp5=max(5,6)=6.
w=4: val=dp0+6=6 -> dp4=max(5? dp4 old5, 6)=6.
dp: 0,0,0,5,6,6,6,11. Correct. Note dp4=6 (item2 alone cost4) vs item1 cost3 value5. Good.

Another: item1 cost3 value5, item2 cost3 value5, X=6.

item2:
w=6: val=dp3+5=10 -> dp6=10.
w=5: val=dp2+5=5 -> dp5=max(5,5)=5.
w=4: val=dp1+5=5 -> dp4=max(5,5)=5.
w=3: val=dp0+5=5 -> dp3=max(5,5)=5.
dp6=10. Correct.

Now, let's think about if dp arrays after at-most DP are nondecreasing but maybe not "max amount with cost at most c" because when updating dp[w], we use dp[w-c] which is max amount with cost at most w-c, but the subset achieving dp[w-c] might have cost t <= w-c. Adding item cost c gives cost t+c <= w. So valid. Conversely, any subset with cost <= w either doesn't use new item (value <= old dp[w]) or uses new item plus subset of previous items with cost <= w-c (value <= old dp[w-c]+a). So recurrence exact. But our arr values are valid achievable amounts with cost <= c? The update ensures arr[w] is achievable with cost <= w. If arr[w] < arr[w-1], arr[w] is still achievable with cost <= w (since arr[w] itself is achievable), but arr[w-1] is also achievable with cost <= w-1 <= w, so the max should be at least arr[w-1]. Running max sets arr[w] to arr[w-1], which is achievable with cost <= w. So it remains valid and improves. Thus prefix max is safe. We can include.

Final code draft:

import sys
from bisect import bisect_left

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, X = data[0], data[1]

    cnt = [0, 0, 0]
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        v = data[idx]
        cnt[v - 1] += 1
        idx += 3

    if cnt[0] == 0 or cnt[1] == 0 or cnt[2] == 0:
        print(0)
        return

    dp = [[0] * (X + 1) for _ in range(3)]
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        v = data[idx]
        a = data[idx + 1]
        c = data[idx + 2]
        idx += 3
        arr = dp[v - 1]
        for w in range(X, c - 1, -1):
            val = arr[w - c] + a
            if val > arr[w]:
                arr[w] = val

    # Ensure dp[c] is nondecreasing (it already is, but this is harmless).
    for arr in dp:
        for i in range(1, X + 1):
            if arr[i] < arr[i - 1]:
                arr[i] = arr[i - 1]

    d0, d1, d2 = dp
    high = min(d0[X], d1[X], d2[X]) + 1

    def ok(m):
        total = 0
        for arr in (d0, d1, d2):
            pos = bisect_left(arr, m)
            if pos > X:
                return False
            total += pos
            if total > X:
                return False
        return True

    lo = 0
    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if ok(mid):
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    print(lo)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Could we use "for w in range(X, c - 1, -1):" but if
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
from bisect import bisect_left

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N, X = data[0], data[1]

    cnt = [0, 0, 0]
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        v = data[idx]
        cnt[v - 1] += 1
        idx += 3

    if cnt[0] == 0 or cnt[1] == 0 or cnt[2] == 0:
        print(0)
        return

    dp = [[0] * (X + 1) for _ in range(3)]

    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        v = data[idx]
        a = data[idx + 1]
        c = data[idx + 2]
        idx += 3

        arr = dp[v - 1]
        for w in range(X, c - 1, -1):
            val = arr[w - c] + a
            if val > arr[w]:
                arr[w] = val

    # Ensure dp[c] is nondecreasing.
    for arr in dp:
        for i in range(1, X + 1):
            if arr[i] < arr[i - 1]:
                arr[i] = arr[i - 1]

    d0, d1, d2 = dp

    def ok(m):
        total = 0
        for arr in (d0, d1, d2):
            pos = bisect_left(arr, m)
            if pos > X:
                return False
            total += pos
            if total > X:
                return False
        return True

    lo = 0
    hi = min(d0[X], d1[X], d2[X]) + 1

    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if ok(mid):
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    print(lo)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
